    1: import OpenAI from "openai";
    2: import { NextResponse } from "next/server";
    3: 
    4: import type {
    5:   AssistantAction,
    6:   AssistantDifficulty,
    7:   AssistantMode,
    8:   AssistantRequestMessage,
    9:   AssistantResourceType,
   10:   QuizGeneratorAction,
   11:   ResourceBuilderAction,
   12: } from "@/types/assistant";
   13: 
   14: const ASSISTANT_MODES: AssistantMode[] = [
   15:   "general",
   16:   "lesson-planner",
   17:   "resource-creator",
   18:   "subject-expert",
   19:   "intervention-coach",
   20:   "examiner",
   21:   "parent-report",
   22: ];
   23: 
   24: type AssistantChatRequest = {
   25:   message: string;
   26:   mode: AssistantMode;
   27:   conversation: AssistantRequestMessage[];
   28:   useDemo?: boolean;
   29: };
   30: 
   31: type OpenAIErrorLike = {
   32:   status?: number;
   33:   code?: string;
   34:   type?: string;
   35:   message?: string;
   36:   error?: {
   37:     code?: string;
   38:     type?: string;
   39:     message?: string;
   40:   };
   41: };
   42: 
   43: type ParsedTeachingRequest = {
   44:   topic: string;
   45:   yearGroup: string;
   46:   qualification: string;
   47:   examBoard: string;
   48:   duration: number;
   49:   difficulty: AssistantDifficulty;
   50:   questionCount: number;
   51:   resourceType: AssistantResourceType | null;
   52:   quizRequested: boolean;
   53: };
   54: 
   55: function isAssistantMode(value: unknown): value is AssistantMode {
   56:   return (
   57:     typeof value === "string" &&
   58:     ASSISTANT_MODES.includes(value as AssistantMode)
   59:   );
   60: }
   61: 
   62: function isConversation(value: unknown): value is AssistantRequestMessage[] {
   63:   if (!Array.isArray(value) || value.length > 20) {
   64:     return false;
   65:   }
   66: 
   67:   return value.every((message) => {
   68:     if (!message || typeof message !== "object") {
   69:       return false;
   70:     }
   71: 
   72:     const candidate = message as Partial<AssistantRequestMessage>;
   73: 
   74:     return (
   75:       (candidate.role === "user" || candidate.role === "assistant") &&
   76:       typeof candidate.content === "string" &&
   77:       candidate.content.trim().length > 0 &&
   78:       candidate.content.length <= 8000
   79:     );
   80:   });
   81: }
   82: 
   83: function isValidRequest(value: unknown): value is AssistantChatRequest {
   84:   if (!value || typeof value !== "object") {
   85:     return false;
   86:   }
   87: 
   88:   const request = value as Partial<AssistantChatRequest>;
   89: 
   90:   return (
   91:     typeof request.message === "string" &&
   92:     request.message.trim().length > 0 &&
   93:     request.message.length <= 8000 &&
   94:     isAssistantMode(request.mode) &&
   95:     isConversation(request.conversation) &&
   96:     (request.useDemo === undefined || typeof request.useDemo === "boolean")
   97:   );
   98: }
   99: 
  100: function getModeInstructions(mode: AssistantMode): string {
  101:   const instructions: Record<AssistantMode, string> = {
  102:     general:
  103:       "Act as a practical teaching assistant. Help with planning, explanations, assessment, differentiation and classroom decisions.",
  104:     "lesson-planner":
  105:       "Create coherent lesson plans with measurable objectives, retrieval, explicit instruction, guided practice, independent practice, assessment and plenary.",
  106:     "resource-creator":
  107:       "Create classroom-ready worksheets, homework, starters, retrieval activities, exit tickets and teaching resources.",
  108:     "subject-expert":
  109:       "Explain Computer Science accurately using age-appropriate language, worked examples, misconceptions and checks for understanding.",
  110:     "intervention-coach":
  111:       "Recommend targeted interventions, scaffolding, retrieval practice, reassessment and manageable next steps for struggling learners.",
  112:     examiner:
  113:       "Act as a careful UK Computer Science examiner. Apply marks fairly, explain awarded marks, identify improvements and provide a model response.",
  114:     "parent-report":
  115:       "Write clear, professional and parent-friendly progress comments. Be evidence-led, constructive and free from unnecessary jargon.",
  116:   };
  117: 
  118:   return instructions[mode];
  119: }
  120: 
  121: function titleCase(value: string): string {
  122:   return value
  123:     .trim()
  124:     .replace(/\s+/g, " ")
  125:     .replace(/\b\w/g, (character) => character.toUpperCase());
  126: }
  127: 
  128: function extractDuration(message: string): number {
  129:   const match = message.match(
  130:     /\b(\d{1,3})\s*(?:-|â€“)?\s*(?:minute|minutes|min|mins)\b/i,
  131:   );
  132: 
  133:   const duration = match ? Number(match[1]) : 50;
  134: 
  135:   return Number.isInteger(duration) && duration >= 5 && duration <= 180
  136:     ? duration
  137:     : 50;
  138: }
  139: 
  140: function extractYearGroup(message: string): string {
  141:   const yearMatch = message.match(/\byear\s*(7|8|9|10|11|12|13)\b/i);
  142: 
  143:   if (yearMatch) {
  144:     return `Year ${yearMatch[1]}`;
  145:   }
  146: 
  147:   if (/\ba[\s-]?level\b/i.test(message)) {
  148:     return "A Level";
  149:   }
  150: 
  151:   if (/\bgcse\b/i.test(message)) {
  152:     return "GCSE";
  153:   }
  154: 
  155:   return "Year 10";
  156: }
  157: 
  158: function extractQualification(message: string): string {
  159:   return /\ba[\s-]?level\b/i.test(message) ? "A Level" : "GCSE";
  160: }
  161: 
  162: function extractExamBoard(message: string): string {
  163:   if (/\baqa\b/i.test(message)) return "AQA";
  164:   if (/\bocr\b/i.test(message)) return "OCR";
  165:   if (/\b(?:pearson\s+)?edexcel\b/i.test(message)) return "Pearson Edexcel";
  166:   if (/\bwjec\b/i.test(message)) return "WJEC";
  167:   if (/\beduqas\b/i.test(message)) return "Eduqas";
  168:   if (/\bcambridge(?:\s+international)?\b/i.test(message)) {
  169:     return "Cambridge International";
  170:   }
  171: 
  172:   return "AQA";
  173: }
  174: 
  175: function extractDifficulty(message: string): AssistantDifficulty {
  176:   if (/\b(?:higher|advanced|challenging|stretch)\b/i.test(message)) {
  177:     return "higher";
  178:   }
  179: 
  180:   if (/\b(?:foundation|accessible|basic|support)\b/i.test(message)) {
  181:     return "foundation";
  182:   }
  183: 
  184:   return "standard";
  185: }
  186: 
  187: function extractQuestionCount(message: string): number {
  188:   const match = message.match(
  189:     /\b(\d{1,2})\s*(?:-|â€“)?\s*(?:question|questions)\b/i,
  190:   );
  191: 
  192:   const count = match ? Number(match[1]) : 10;
  193: 
  194:   return Number.isInteger(count) && count >= 3 && count <= 20 ? count : 10;
  195: }
  196: 
  197: function isQuizRequest(message: string): boolean {
  198:   return /\bquiz\b/i.test(message) && !/\bretrieval\s+quiz\b/i.test(message);
  199: }
  200: 
  201: function detectResourceType(
  202:   message: string,
  203:   mode: AssistantMode,
  204: ): AssistantResourceType | null {
  205:   const normalised = message.toLowerCase();
  206: 
  207:   if (
  208:     normalised.includes("retrieval quiz") ||
  209:     normalised.includes("retrieval questions")
  210:   ) {
  211:     return "retrieval-quiz";
  212:   }
  213: 
  214:   if (
  215:     normalised.includes("exit ticket") ||
  216:     normalised.includes("exit question")
  217:   ) {
  218:     return "exit-ticket";
  219:   }
  220: 
  221:   if (normalised.includes("worksheet")) return "worksheet";
  222:   if (normalised.includes("homework")) return "homework";
  223:   if (normalised.includes("starter") || normalised.includes("bell task")) {
  224:     return "starter";
  225:   }
  226: 
  227:   if (normalised.includes("lesson") || mode === "lesson-planner") {
  228:     return "lesson-plan";
  229:   }
  230: 
  231:   return null;
  232: }
  233: 
  234: function removePlanningWords(message: string): string {
  235:   return message
  236:     .replace(
  237:       /^(please\s+)?(can\s+you\s+)?(create|generate|write|plan|make|produce|design|build)\s+/i,
  238:       "",
  239:     )
  240:     .replace(/^(a|an|the)\s+/i, "")
  241:     .replace(/\b\d{1,3}\s*(?:-|â€“)?\s*(?:minute|minutes|min|mins)\b/gi, "")
  242:     .replace(/\b\d{1,2}\s*(?:-|â€“)?\s*(?:question|questions)\b/gi, "")
  243:     .replace(/\b(?:year\s*(?:7|8|9|10|11|12|13)|gcse|a[\s-]?level)\b/gi, "")
  244:     .replace(
  245:       /\b(?:aqa|ocr|pearson\s+edexcel|edexcel|wjec|eduqas|cambridge\s+international)\b/gi,
  246:       "",
  247:     )
  248:     .replace(
  249:       /\b(?:foundation|standard|higher|advanced|challenging|stretch|accessible|basic)\b/gi,
  250:       "",
  251:     )
  252:     .replace(
  253:       /\b(?:complete\s+)?(?:lesson\s+plan|lesson|worksheet|homework|starter\s+activity|starter|retrieval\s+quiz|retrieval\s+questions|exit\s+ticket|quiz)\b/gi,
  254:       "",
  255:     )
  256:     .replace(/\b(?:for|on|about|covering|focused\s+on)\b/gi, " ")
  257:     .replace(/[.?!]+$/g, "")
  258:     .replace(/\s+/g, " ")
  259:     .trim();
  260: }
  261: 
  262: function extractTopic(message: string): string {
  263:   return titleCase(removePlanningWords(message) || "Computer Science");
  264: }
  265: 
  266: function parseTeachingRequest(
  267:   message: string,
  268:   mode: AssistantMode,
  269: ): ParsedTeachingRequest {
  270:   return {
  271:     topic: extractTopic(message),
  272:     yearGroup: extractYearGroup(message),
  273:     qualification: extractQualification(message),
  274:     examBoard: extractExamBoard(message),
  275:     duration: extractDuration(message),
  276:     difficulty: extractDifficulty(message),
  277:     questionCount: extractQuestionCount(message),
  278:     resourceType: detectResourceType(message, mode),
  279:     quizRequested: isQuizRequest(message),
  280:   };
  281: }
  282: 
  283: function createResourceBuilderAction(
  284:   parsed: ParsedTeachingRequest,
  285: ): ResourceBuilderAction | undefined {
  286:   if (!parsed.resourceType || parsed.quizRequested) {
  287:     return undefined;
  288:   }
  289: 
  290:   const names: Record<AssistantResourceType, string> = {
  291:     "lesson-plan": "lesson plan",
  292:     starter: "starter activity",
  293:     "retrieval-quiz": "retrieval quiz",
  294:     worksheet: "worksheet",
  295:     homework: "homework task",
  296:     "exit-ticket": "exit ticket",
  297:   };
  298: 
  299:   return {
  300:     type: "open-resource-builder",
  301:     resourceType: parsed.resourceType,
  302:     topic: parsed.topic,
  303:     yearGroup: parsed.yearGroup,
  304:     examBoard: parsed.examBoard,
  305:     duration: parsed.duration,
  306:     difficulty: parsed.difficulty,
  307:     objectives: "",
  308:     additionalNotes: `Create a ${names[parsed.resourceType]} based on the Copilot request. Review and adapt all generated content before publishing.`,
  309:     buttonLabel: `Open ${titleCase(names[parsed.resourceType])} in Builder`,
  310:   };
  311: }
  312: 
  313: function createQuizGeneratorAction(
  314:   parsed: ParsedTeachingRequest,
  315: ): QuizGeneratorAction | undefined {
  316:   if (!parsed.quizRequested) {
  317:     return undefined;
  318:   }
  319: 
  320:   return {
  321:     type: "open-quiz-generator",
  322:     topic: parsed.topic,
  323:     qualification: parsed.qualification,
  324:     examBoard: parsed.examBoard,
  325:     difficulty: parsed.difficulty,
  326:     questionCount: parsed.questionCount,
  327:     buttonLabel: "Open Quiz Generator",
  328:   };
  329: }
  330: 
  331: function createAction(
  332:   parsed: ParsedTeachingRequest,
  333: ): AssistantAction | undefined {
  334:   return (
  335:     createQuizGeneratorAction(parsed) || createResourceBuilderAction(parsed)
  336:   );
  337: }
  338: 
  339: function createDemoResponse(parsed: ParsedTeachingRequest): string {
  340:   if (parsed.quizRequested) {
  341:     return [
  342:       `## ${parsed.questionCount}-question quiz: ${parsed.topic}`,
  343:       "",
  344:       `The settings are ready for a ${parsed.qualification} ${parsed.examBoard} quiz at ${parsed.difficulty} difficulty.`,
  345:       "",
  346:       "**Next step**",
  347:       "Open the Quiz Generator, review the prefilled settings and choose live AI generation or the no-cost demo generator.",
  348:     ].join("\n");
  349:   }
  350: 
  351:   if (parsed.resourceType === "lesson-plan") {
  352:     return [
  353:       `## ${parsed.duration}-minute lesson: ${parsed.topic}`,
  354:       "",
  355:       "**Learning objectives**",
  356:       `1. Explain the key principles of ${parsed.topic}.`,
  357:       `2. Apply knowledge of ${parsed.topic} to worked examples.`,
  358:       `3. Evaluate common mistakes involving ${parsed.topic}.`,
  359:       "",
  360:       "**Next step**",
  361:       "Open the Resource Builder to review the prefilled settings and generate the complete lesson.",
  362:     ].join("\n");
  363:   }
  364: 
  365:   return [
  366:     `## ${parsed.topic}`,
  367:     "",
  368:     `${parsed.topic} is an important Computer Science concept.`,
  369:     "",
  370:     "Use accurate terminology, a worked example and a short check for understanding.",
  371:   ].join("\n");
  372: }
  373: 
  374: function getErrorDetails(error: unknown): OpenAIErrorLike {
  375:   return error && typeof error === "object" ? (error as OpenAIErrorLike) : {};
  376: }
  377: 
  378: function isQuotaError(error: unknown): boolean {
  379:   const details = getErrorDetails(error);
  380:   const text = [
  381:     details.code,
  382:     details.type,
  383:     details.message,
  384:     details.error?.code,
  385:     details.error?.type,
  386:     details.error?.message,
  387:   ]
  388:     .filter(Boolean)
  389:     .join(" ")
  390:     .toLowerCase();
  391: 
  392:   return (
  393:     details.status === 429 ||
  394:     text.includes("insufficient_quota") ||
  395:     text.includes("exceeded your current quota") ||
  396:     text.includes("billing")
  397:   );
  398: }
  399: 
  400: function successfulResponse({
  401:   message,
  402:   source,
  403:   action,
  404:   warning,
  405: }: {
  406:   message: string;
  407:   source: "ai" | "demo";
  408:   action?: AssistantAction;
  409:   warning?: string;
  410: }) {
  411:   return NextResponse.json({ message, source, action, warning });
  412: }
  413: 
  414: export async function POST(request: Request) {
  415:   try {
  416:     const body: unknown = await request.json();
  417: 
  418:     if (!isValidRequest(body)) {
  419:       return NextResponse.json(
  420:         {
  421:           error: "Invalid assistant request.",
  422:           errorCode: "invalid_request",
  423:         },
  424:         { status: 400 },
  425:       );
  426:     }
  427: 
  428:     const { message, mode, conversation, useDemo } = body;
  429:     const parsed = parseTeachingRequest(message, mode);
  430:     const action = createAction(parsed);
  431:     const apiKey = process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY;
  432: 
  433:     if (useDemo || !apiKey) {
  434:       return successfulResponse({
  435:         message: createDemoResponse(parsed),
  436:         source: "demo",
  437:         action,
  438:         warning: !apiKey
  439:           ? "Live AI is not configured, so a demo response was generated."
  440:           : undefined,
  441:       });
  442:     }
  443: 
  444:     try {
  445:       const openai = new OpenAI({ apiKey });
  446:       const model = process.env.OPENAI_ASSISTANT_MODEL || "gpt-5.6";
  447: 
  448:       const response = await openai.responses.create({
  449:         model,
  450:         instructions: [
  451:           "You are CS Master Copilot, an expert UK secondary Computer Science teaching assistant.",
  452:           "Use British English.",
  453:           "Give accurate, practical and classroom-ready support.",
  454:           "Use clear headings and concise sections.",
  455:           "Use correct Computer Science terminology.",
  456:           "Do not invent exam-board requirements.",
  457:           `The interpreted topic is: ${parsed.topic}.`,
  458:           `The interpreted qualification is: ${parsed.qualification}.`,
  459:           `The interpreted exam board is: ${parsed.examBoard}.`,
  460:           `The interpreted difficulty is: ${parsed.difficulty}.`,
  461:           getModeInstructions(mode),
  462:         ].join(" "),
  463:         input: [
  464:           ...conversation.map((item) => ({
  465:             role: item.role,
  466:             content: item.content,
  467:           })),
  468:           {
  469:             role: "user" as const,
  470:             content: message.trim(),
  471:           },
  472:         ],
  473:       });
  474: 
  475:       if (!response.output_text) {
  476:         return successfulResponse({
  477:           message: createDemoResponse(parsed),
  478:           source: "demo",
  479:           action,
  480:           warning:
  481:             "The live assistant returned no text, so a demo response was used.",
  482:         });
  483:       }
  484: 
  485:       return successfulResponse({
  486:         message: response.output_text,
  487:         source: "ai",
  488:         action,
  489:       });
  490:     } catch (aiError) {
  491:       console.error("Live assistant error:", aiError);
  492: 
  493:       const details = getErrorDetails(aiError);
  494:       let warning =
  495:         "The live assistant was unavailable. A demo response was generated instead.";
  496: 
  497:       if (isQuotaError(aiError)) {
  498:         warning =
  499:           "The OpenAI account currently has no available quota. A demo response was generated instead.";
  500:       } else if (details.status === 401) {
  501:         warning =
  502:           "The configured API key was rejected. A demo response was generated instead.";
  503:       }
  504: 
  505:       return successfulResponse({
  506:         message: createDemoResponse(parsed),
  507:         source: "demo",
  508:         action,
  509:         warning,
  510:       });
  511:     }
  512:   } catch (error) {
  513:     console.error("Assistant route error:", error);
  514: 
  515:     return NextResponse.json(
  516:       {
  517:         error: "The assistant request could not be processed.",
  518:         errorCode: "assistant_failed",
  519:       },
  520:       { status: 500 },
  521:     );
  522:   }
  523: }

FILE: app\api\ai\generate-exam-questions\route.ts
==============================================================================================================
    1: import OpenAI from "openai";
    2: import { NextResponse } from "next/server";
    3: 
    4: import type {
    5:   AssessmentBlueprintItem,
    6:   AssessmentGenerationMode,
    7:   AssessmentObjective,
    8:   AssessmentQuestionType,
    9:   ExamQuestionCommandWord,
   10:   ExamQuestionDifficulty,
   11:   ExamQuestionLevelDescriptor,
   12:   ExamQuestionMarkPoint,
   13:   GeneratedExamQuestion,
   14:   GeneratedExamQuestionSet,
   15: } from "@/types/examQuestion";
   16: 
   17: type RequestBody = {
   18:   topic: string;
   19:   qualification: string;
   20:   examBoard: string;
   21:   difficulty: ExamQuestionDifficulty;
   22:   generationMode: AssessmentGenerationMode;
   23:   blueprint: AssessmentBlueprintItem[];
   24:   useDemo?: boolean;
   25: };
   26: 
   27: type ParsedQuestion = {
   28:   question: string;
   29:   context: string;
   30:   markScheme: Array<{
   31:     description: string;
   32:     marks: number;
   33:   }>;
   34:   levelDescriptors: Array<{
   35:     level: number;
   36:     markRange: string;
   37:     description: string;
   38:   }>;
   39:   modelAnswer: string;
   40:   examinerGuidance: string[];
   41:   commonMisconceptions: string[];
   42: };
   43: 
   44: const QUESTION_TYPES: AssessmentQuestionType[] = [
   45:   "multiple-choice",
   46:   "state-identify",
   47:   "short-response",
   48:   "definition",
   49:   "conversion",
   50:   "calculation",
   51:   "worked-calculation",
   52:   "complete-table",
   53:   "trace-table",
   54:   "truth-table",
   55:   "code-completion",
   56:   "code-tracing",
   57:   "debugging",
   58:   "algorithm-design",
   59:   "compare",
   60:   "explain",
   61:   "scenario-application",
   62:   "extended-response",
   63:   "discuss",
   64:   "evaluate",
   65: ];
   66: 
   67: const COMMAND_WORDS: ExamQuestionCommandWord[] = [
   68:   "state",
   69:   "identify",
   70:   "define",
   71:   "describe",
   72:   "explain",
   73:   "compare",
   74:   "calculate",
   75:   "complete",
   76:   "write",
   77:   "trace",
   78:   "debug",
   79:   "design",
   80:   "evaluate",
   81:   "discuss",
   82: ];
   83: 
   84: function isDifficulty(value: unknown): value is ExamQuestionDifficulty {
   85:   return value === "foundation" || value === "standard" || value === "higher";
   86: }
   87: 
   88: function isObjective(value: unknown): value is AssessmentObjective {
   89:   return value === "AO1" || value === "AO2" || value === "AO3";
   90: }
   91: 
   92: function isQuestionType(value: unknown): value is AssessmentQuestionType {
   93:   return (
   94:     typeof value === "string" &&
   95:     QUESTION_TYPES.includes(value as AssessmentQuestionType)
   96:   );
   97: }
   98: 
   99: function isCommandWord(value: unknown): value is ExamQuestionCommandWord {
  100:   return (
  101:     typeof value === "string" &&
  102:     COMMAND_WORDS.includes(value as ExamQuestionCommandWord)
  103:   );
  104: }
  105: 
  106: function isBlueprintItem(value: unknown): value is AssessmentBlueprintItem {
  107:   if (!value || typeof value !== "object") {
  108:     return false;
  109:   }
  110: 
  111:   const item = value as Partial<AssessmentBlueprintItem>;
  112: 
  113:   return (
  114:     typeof item.id === "string" &&
  115:     typeof item.questionNumber === "number" &&
  116:     Number.isInteger(item.questionNumber) &&
  117:     item.questionNumber >= 1 &&
  118:     isObjective(item.assessmentObjective) &&
  119:     isQuestionType(item.questionType) &&
  120:     isCommandWord(item.commandWord) &&
  121:     typeof item.marks === "number" &&
  122:     Number.isInteger(item.marks) &&
  123:     item.marks >= 1 &&
  124:     item.marks <= 12 &&
  125:     isDifficulty(item.difficulty) &&
  126:     typeof item.topicFocus === "string" &&
  127:     item.topicFocus.trim().length > 0
  128:   );
  129: }
  130: 
  131: function isValidRequest(value: unknown): value is RequestBody {
  132:   if (!value || typeof value !== "object") {
  133:     return false;
  134:   }
  135: 
  136:   const request = value as Partial<RequestBody>;
  137: 
  138:   return (
  139:     typeof request.topic === "string" &&
  140:     request.topic.trim().length > 0 &&
  141:     typeof request.qualification === "string" &&
  142:     request.qualification.trim().length > 0 &&
  143:     typeof request.examBoard === "string" &&
  144:     request.examBoard.trim().length > 0 &&
  145:     isDifficulty(request.difficulty) &&
  146:     (request.generationMode === "automatic" ||
  147:       request.generationMode === "manual") &&
  148:     Array.isArray(request.blueprint) &&
  149:     request.blueprint.length >= 1 &&
  150:     request.blueprint.length <= 20 &&
  151:     request.blueprint.every(isBlueprintItem) &&
  152:     (request.useDemo === undefined || typeof request.useDemo === "boolean")
  153:   );
  154: }
  155: 
  156: function slugify(value: string): string {
  157:   return value
  158:     .trim()
  159:     .toLowerCase()
  160:     .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, "-")
  161:     .replace(/^-+|-+$/g, "");
  162: }
  163: 
  164: function titleCase(value: string): string {
  165:   return value
  166:     .trim()
  167:     .replace(/\s+/g, " ")
  168:     .replace(/\b\w/g, (character) => character.toUpperCase());
  169: }
  170: 
  171: function createLevelDescriptors(marks: number): ExamQuestionLevelDescriptor[] {
  172:   if (marks < 6) {
  173:     return [];
  174:   }
  175: 
  176:   if (marks <= 8) {
  177:     return [
  178:       {
  179:         level: 3,
  180:         markRange: `${Math.max(6, marks - 2)}â€“${marks}`,
  181:         description:
  182:           "A detailed, accurate and well-developed response with clear application or evaluation and a supported conclusion where required.",
  183:       },
  184:       {
  185:         level: 2,
  186:         markRange: `3â€“${Math.max(4, marks - 3)}`,
  187:         description:
  188:           "A mostly accurate response with some developed reasoning, although coverage or evaluation may be uneven.",
  189:       },
  190:       {
  191:         level: 1,
  192:         markRange: "1â€“2",
  193:         description:
  194:           "Basic relevant statements with limited development, application or technical precision.",
  195:       },
  196:     ];
  197:   }
  198: 
  199:   return [
  200:     {
  201:       level: 4,
  202:       markRange: `${Math.max(10, marks - 2)}â€“${marks}`,
  203:       description:
  204:         "A comprehensive, technically accurate and balanced response with sustained reasoning, effective use of examples and a justified conclusion.",
  205:     },
  206:     {
  207:       level: 3,
  208:       markRange: `7â€“${Math.max(9, marks - 3)}`,
  209:       description:
  210:         "A mostly accurate and developed response with relevant analysis or evaluation, though balance or depth may be inconsistent.",
  211:     },
  212:     {
  213:       level: 2,
  214:       markRange: "4â€“6",
  215:       description:
  216:         "Some accurate knowledge and application, but development is limited and reasoning may be incomplete or one-sided.",
  217:     },
  218:     {
  219:       level: 1,
  220:       markRange: "1â€“3",
  221:       description:
  222:         "Basic relevant statements with little explanation, application or evaluation.",
  223:     },
  224:   ];
  225: }
  226: 
  227: function createSpecificMarkScheme(
  228:   item: AssessmentBlueprintItem,
  229:   points: string[],
  230: ): ExamQuestionMarkPoint[] {
  231:   if (item.marks >= 6) {
  232:     return points
  233:       .slice(0, Math.min(points.length, item.marks))
  234:       .map((description, index) => ({
  235:         id: `${item.id}-mark-${index + 1}`,
  236:         description,
  237:         marks: 1,
  238:       }));
  239:   }
  240: 
  241:   const selected = points.slice(0, item.marks);
  242: 
  243:   while (selected.length < item.marks) {
  244:     selected.push(
  245:       `Credit a further accurate, relevant and distinct point about ${item.topicFocus}.`,
  246:     );
  247:   }
  248: 
  249:   return selected.map((description, index) => ({
  250:     id: `${item.id}-mark-${index + 1}`,
  251:     description,
  252:     marks: 1,
  253:   }));
  254: }
  255: 
  256: function demoContentForType(
  257:   item: AssessmentBlueprintItem,
  258:   index: number,
  259: ): {
  260:   question: string;
  261:   context: string;
  262:   markPoints: string[];
  263:   modelAnswer: string;
  264:   examinerGuidance: string[];
  265:   misconceptions: string[];
  266: } {
  267:   const topic = item.topicFocus.trim();
  268: 
  269:   const variant = index + 1;
  270: 
  271:   switch (item.questionType) {
  272:     case "multiple-choice":
  273:       return {
  274:         question: `Which option gives the most accurate statement about ${topic}?\n\nA. ${topic} is used only for storing images.\nB. ${topic} is a Computer Science concept that can be applied to solve or represent problems.\nC. ${topic} removes the need for algorithms.\nD. ${topic} prevents computers from processing data.`,
  275:         context: "Select one answer.",
  276:         markPoints: [
  277:           `Award 1 mark for selecting option B: ${topic} is a Computer Science concept that can be applied to solve or represent problems.`,
  278:         ],
  279:         modelAnswer: "B",
  280:         examinerGuidance: [
  281:           "Award the mark only for the correct option.",
  282:           "Do not award multiple selections unless the final answer is clearly indicated.",
  283:         ],
  284:         misconceptions: [
  285:           `Believing that ${topic} has only one narrow use.`,
  286:           "Selecting an option because it contains familiar terminology rather than checking its accuracy.",
  287:         ],
  288:       };
  289: 
  290:     case "conversion":
  291:       return {
  292:         question: `Convert the value connected to ${topic} from one appropriate representation to another. Show each stage of your working.`,
  293:         context: `Use the value ${variant * 7 + 18} as the source value. The teacher may edit the representations before publishing.`,
  294:         markPoints: [
  295:           "Correctly identifies the place values or conversion method.",
  296:           "Shows an accurate intermediate stage.",
  297:           "Produces the correct final representation.",
  298:           "Uses the correct base, units or notation.",
  299:         ],
  300:         modelAnswer: `A complete answer should show the place values or repeated-division method, record the intermediate values accurately and give the final converted value with the correct notation.`,
  301:         examinerGuidance: [
  302:           "Award method marks where the process is correct but a later arithmetic slip occurs.",
  303:           "Require the final answer to use unambiguous notation.",
  304:         ],
  305:         misconceptions: [
  306:           "Reading a representation as if it were denary without considering its base.",
  307:           "Omitting leading zeros or notation when these are required by the question.",
  308:         ],
  309:       };
  310: 
  311:     case "calculation":
  312:     case "worked-calculation":
  313:       return {
  314:         question: `${titleCase(
  315:           item.commandWord,
  316:         )} a calculation involving ${topic}. Show all working and identify any important condition or error that could occur.`,
  317:         context: `Use two original values suitable for ${item.difficulty} difficulty. This is demo content, so the teacher should insert or edit the final numerical values.`,
  318:         markPoints: [
  319:           "Uses the correct method or operation.",
  320:           "Shows the first accurate stage of working.",
  321:           "Completes the remaining working accurately.",
  322:           "States the correct final answer.",
  323:           "Identifies a relevant condition such as overflow, range or validation where applicable.",
  324:           "Explains the effect of the identified condition.",
  325:         ],
  326:         modelAnswer: `The response should show the chosen values, apply the correct operation step by step, state the final result and explain any relevant limitation such as range or overflow.`,
  327:         examinerGuidance: [
  328:           "Credit correct working even if the final answer contains a single arithmetic slip.",
  329:           "Do not award a method mark for an unexplained guess.",
  330:         ],
  331:         misconceptions: [
  332:           "Applying a denary method without adapting it to the representation being used.",
  333:           "Ignoring carry, borrow, range or overflow conditions.",
  334:         ],
  335:       };
  336: 
  337:     case "complete-table":
  338:     case "truth-table":
  339:       return {
  340:         question: `Complete the missing entries in a table that assesses ${topic}.`,
  341:         context: `The final published version should contain ${Math.max(
  342:           2,
  343:           Math.min(8, item.marks),
  344:         )} missing cells and enough headings to make every required output clear.`,
  345:         markPoints: [
  346:           "Completes the first required row or cell accurately.",
  347:           "Completes the second required row or cell accurately.",
  348:           "Completes the third required row or cell accurately.",
  349:           "Completes the fourth required row or cell accurately.",
  350:           "Uses consistent notation throughout the table.",
  351:           "Derives the final output from the correct preceding values.",
  352:         ],
  353:         modelAnswer:
  354:           "The model answer should reproduce the completed table and briefly show how each output was obtained.",
  355:         examinerGuidance: [
  356:           "Award marks independently for each correct cell unless one error is carried forward consistently.",
  357:           "Accept equivalent Boolean notation where it is unambiguous.",
  358:         ],
  359:         misconceptions: [
  360:           "Treating AND and OR as if they produce the same output.",
  361:           "Changing an earlier input while calculating a later row.",
  362:         ],
  363:       };
  364: 
  365:     case "trace-table":
  366:     case "code-tracing":
  367:       return {
  368:         question: `Trace the supplied algorithm or code connected to ${topic} and record the value of each variable after every iteration.`,
  369:         context: `Use an original short algorithm with ${Math.max(
  370:           2,
  371:           Math.min(6, item.marks),
  372:         )} meaningful state changes.`,
  373:         markPoints: [
  374:           "Records the correct initial variable values.",
  375:           "Records the correct values after the first iteration.",
  376:           "Records the correct values after the second iteration.",
  377:           "Continues the trace accurately for the remaining iterations.",
  378:           "States the correct final output.",
  379:           "Uses a clear and consistent trace-table layout.",
  380:         ],
  381:         modelAnswer:
  382:           "The model answer should contain a fully completed trace table with one row per iteration and the correct final output.",
  383:         examinerGuidance: [
  384:           "Award follow-through marks when one early error is applied consistently.",
  385:           "Do not credit a final output with no supporting trace when the question requires working.",
  386:         ],
  387:         misconceptions: [
  388:           "Updating variables in the wrong order.",
  389:           "Forgetting that a loop condition is checked before or after an iteration.",
  390:         ],
  391:       };
  392: 
  393:     case "code-completion":
  394:       return {
  395:         question: `Complete the missing lines in an original program that uses ${topic}.`,
  396:         context:
  397:           "The code should use clear variable names and contain only the number of gaps justified by the mark allocation.",
  398:         markPoints: [
  399:           "Uses a correct statement or expression in the first gap.",
  400:           "Uses a correct statement or expression in the second gap.",
  401:           "Uses appropriate syntax for the selected programming language.",
  402:           "Preserves the intended control flow.",
  403:           "Produces the required output for the stated test data.",
  404:         ],
  405:         modelAnswer:
  406:           "The model answer should provide the completed code and a short explanation of how the inserted statements satisfy the requirements.",
  407:         examinerGuidance: [
  408:           "Accept logically equivalent code.",
  409:           "Ignore minor syntax errors only where the intended logic remains completely clear and the mark scheme permits it.",
  410:         ],
  411:         misconceptions: [
  412:           "Using assignment where comparison is required.",
  413:           "Placing a statement outside the required loop or selection block.",
  414:         ],
  415:       };
  416: 
  417:     case "debugging":
  418:       return {
  419:         question: `An original solution involving ${topic} contains errors. Identify the errors, correct them and explain why each correction is needed.`,
  420:         context: `Include at least ${Math.max(
  421:           1,
  422:           Math.min(4, Math.floor(item.marks / 2)),
  423:         )} distinct logical or syntax errors.`,
  424:         markPoints: [
  425:           "Correctly identifies the first error.",
  426:           "Provides a valid correction for the first error.",
  427:           "Correctly identifies the second error.",
  428:           "Provides a valid correction for the second error.",
  429:           "Explains how the corrected solution now meets the requirement.",
  430:           "Uses appropriate technical terminology.",
  431:         ],
  432:         modelAnswer:
  433:           "The model answer should quote or identify each faulty line, provide corrected code or logic and explain the effect of every correction.",
  434:         examinerGuidance: [
  435:           "Do not award a correction mark when the replacement creates a different error.",
  436:           "Accept alternative corrections that preserve the required behaviour.",
  437:         ],
  438:         misconceptions: [
  439:           "Changing code without explaining the original error.",
  440:           "Correcting syntax while leaving the underlying logic incorrect.",
  441:         ],
  442:       };
  443: 
  444:     case "algorithm-design":
  445:       return {
  446:         question: `Design an algorithm that applies ${topic} to solve the stated problem. Use pseudocode, structured English or a flowchart.`,
  447:         context: `Create an original scenario requiring input, processing, validation and output appropriate to ${item.difficulty} difficulty.`,
  448:         markPoints: [
  449:           "Identifies or accepts the required input.",
  450:           "Uses an appropriate process connected to the topic.",
  451:           "Uses correct sequencing.",
  452:           "Uses suitable selection or iteration where required.",
  453:           "Produces the required output.",
  454:           "Handles an important boundary or invalid case.",
  455:           "Uses clear variable names and unambiguous logic.",
  456:           "Provides a complete solution that meets the scenario.",
  457:         ],
  458:         modelAnswer:
  459:           "The model answer should provide a complete algorithm with clear inputs, ordered processing, any required selection or iteration, validation and the specified output.",
  460:         examinerGuidance: [
  461:           "Credit any logically correct algorithmic representation.",
  462:           "For higher bands, require a complete and robust solution rather than isolated fragments.",
  463:         ],
  464:         misconceptions: [
  465:           "Describing the desired result without giving an executable sequence of steps.",
  466:           "Using a loop or condition that can never terminate or be satisfied.",
  467:         ],
  468:       };
  469: 
  470:     case "compare":
  471:       return {
  472:         question: `Compare two appropriate approaches, representations or technologies connected to ${topic}.`,
  473:         context:
  474:           "The response must include similarities, differences and a judgement linked to a stated use case.",
  475:         markPoints: [
  476:           "States one accurate similarity.",
  477:           "States one accurate difference.",
  478:           "Develops the first comparison using technical reasoning.",
  479:           "Develops a second comparison using technical reasoning.",
  480:           "Links the comparison to the stated context.",
  481:           "Reaches a justified conclusion.",
  482:         ],
  483:         modelAnswer: `A complete answer should compare both options directly, explain at least two technically accurate similarities or differences, apply them to the scenario and justify which option is more suitable.`,
  484:         examinerGuidance: [
  485:           "Do not award full marks for two separate descriptions with no direct comparison.",
  486:           "A conclusion must follow from the evidence given.",
  487:         ],
  488:         misconceptions: [
  489:           "Describing only one option.",
  490:           "Giving a preference without technical justification.",
  491:         ],
  492:       };
  493: 
  494:     case "scenario-application":
  495:       return {
  496:         question: `Apply your knowledge of ${topic} to the original scenario provided. Explain the most suitable solution and its likely effect.`,
  497:         context: `A fictional organisation must make a decision involving ${topic}. Include constraints such as cost, performance, security or usability.`,
  498:         markPoints: [
  499:           "Identifies a relevant feature of the scenario.",
  500:           "Selects an appropriate concept or solution.",
  501:           "Explains how the solution applies to the scenario.",
  502:           "Explains one likely benefit.",
  503:           "Explains one limitation or risk.",
  504:           "Reaches a justified recommendation.",
  505:         ],
  506:         modelAnswer: `The model answer should select a suitable solution, connect each point explicitly to the scenario, explain benefits and limitations and finish with a justified recommendation.`,
  507:         examinerGuidance: [
  508:           "Generic textbook statements must be applied to the scenario to gain application marks.",
  509:           "Accept different recommendations when they are technically accurate and justified.",
  510:         ],
  511:         misconceptions: [
  512:           "Repeating information from the scenario without applying Computer Science knowledge.",
  513:           "Assuming that the most powerful or expensive option is always the most suitable.",
  514:         ],
  515:       };
  516: 
  517:     case "extended-response":
  518:     case "discuss":
  519:     case "evaluate":
  520:       return {
  521:         question: `${titleCase(
  522:           item.commandWord,
  523:         )} the role, benefits and limitations of ${topic} in the stated computing context. Reach a justified conclusion.`,
  524:         context: `Use an original scenario containing at least two competing considerations relevant to ${topic}.`,
  525:         markPoints: [
  526:           "Demonstrates accurate knowledge of the topic.",
  527:           "Explains a relevant benefit.",
  528:           "Develops the benefit using the scenario.",
  529:           "Explains a relevant limitation or risk.",
  530:           "Develops the limitation using the scenario.",
  531:           "Considers an alternative viewpoint or solution.",
  532:           "Uses accurate technical terminology.",
  533:           "Organises the response into a clear line of reasoning.",
  534:           "Makes a judgement based on the evidence.",
  535:           "Provides a justified conclusion linked to the scenario.",
  536:           "Maintains balance across the response.",
  537:           "Shows sustained analysis or evaluation.",
  538:         ],
  539:         modelAnswer: `A high-level response should explain the relevant principles of ${topic}, apply benefits and limitations to the scenario, consider alternatives and reach a balanced, technically justified conclusion.`,
  540:         examinerGuidance: [
  541:           "Use the level descriptors rather than counting isolated points for the final mark.",
  542:           "A one-sided response cannot access the highest level where balance is required.",
  543:           "Reward sustained reasoning, accurate terminology and scenario-specific judgement.",
  544:         ],
  545:         misconceptions: [
  546:           "Listing advantages and disadvantages without explaining their significance.",
  547:           "Giving a conclusion that is not supported by the preceding discussion.",
  548:         ],
  549:       };
  550: 
  551:     case "definition":
  552:       return {
  553:         question: `Define the term ${topic}.`,
  554:         context: "Use precise Computer Science terminology.",
  555:         markPoints: [
  556:           `States the essential meaning of ${topic}.`,
  557:           "Includes a second defining characteristic or accurate technical detail.",
  558:         ],
  559:         modelAnswer: `A complete definition should state what ${topic} is and include the technical characteristic that distinguishes it from related concepts.`,
  560:         examinerGuidance: [
  561:           "Do not credit an example on its own unless it demonstrates the defining meaning.",
  562:           "Accept equivalent technical wording.",
  563:         ],
  564:         misconceptions: [
  565:           "Giving an example instead of a definition.",
  566:           "Using the term itself within the definition without explaining it.",
  567:         ],
  568:       };
  569: 
  570:     case "explain":
  571:     case "short-response":
  572:     case "state-identify":
  573:     default:
  574:       return {
  575:         question: `${titleCase(
  576:           item.commandWord,
  577:         )} ${item.marks === 1 ? "one accurate feature" : "the key principles"} of ${topic}.`,
  578:         context: `Answer using accurate terminology and an example where appropriate. Variant ${variant} uses a distinct focus within the topic.`,
  579:         markPoints: [
  580:           `States an accurate point about ${topic}.`,
  581:           "Develops the point with a technically accurate explanation.",
  582:           "Provides a relevant example.",
  583:           "Explains how the example demonstrates the concept.",
  584:           "Links the explanation to a computing context.",
  585:           "Uses precise technical terminology.",
  586:         ],
  587:         modelAnswer: `A complete answer should state an accurate principle of ${topic}, explain why or how it works and use a relevant example to demonstrate understanding.`,
  588:         examinerGuidance: [
  589:           "Award separate marks only for distinct ideas.",
  590:           "A repeated point expressed differently should not be credited twice.",
  591:         ],
  592:         misconceptions: [
  593:           "Giving a vague statement without technical explanation.",
  594:           "Providing an example that is unrelated to the stated principle.",
  595:         ],
  596:       };
  597:   }
  598: }
  599: 
  600: type TopicProfile = {
  601:   id: string;
  602:   patterns: RegExp[];
  603:   displayName: string;
  604:   definition: string;
  605:   explanation: string;
  606:   examples: string[];
  607:   benefits: string[];
  608:   limitations: string[];
  609:   misconceptions: string[];
  610:   keywords: string[];
  611: };
  612: 
  613: const TOPIC_PROFILES: TopicProfile[] = [
  614:   {
  615:     id: "binary-arithmetic",
  616:     patterns: [
  617:       /\bbinary\b/i,

 1382: 
 1383: function resolveTopicProfile(topic: string): TopicProfile {
 1384:   const matched = TOPIC_PROFILES.find((profile) =>
 1385:     profile.patterns.some((pattern) => pattern.test(topic)),
 1386:   );
 1387: 
 1388:   if (matched) {
 1389:     return matched;
 1390:   }
 1391: 
 1392:   return {
 1393:     id: "generic-computing",
 1394:     patterns: [],
 1395:     displayName: topic,
 1396:     definition: `${topic} is a Computer Science concept that should be defined using its purpose, main characteristics and relationship to a computing system or problem.`,
 1397:     explanation: `${topic} should be explained by identifying what it does, how it works, where it is used and the effect it has on data, software, hardware, users or a wider computing system.`,
 1398:     examples: [
 1399:       `a practical computing use of ${topic}`,
 1400:       `a scenario in which ${topic} solves or influences a problem`,
 1401:       `a comparison between ${topic} and an alternative approach`,
 1402:     ],
 1403:     benefits: [
 1404:       `a relevant advantage of ${topic} in the stated context`,
 1405:       "improved efficiency, accuracy, reliability, security or usability where technically appropriate",
 1406:       "support for solving the identified computing problem",
 1407:     ],
 1408:     limitations: [
 1409:       `a relevant technical or practical limitation of ${topic}`,
 1410:       "cost, complexity, security, reliability or usability constraints where appropriate",
 1411:       "dependence on correct design, implementation and testing",
 1412:     ],
 1413:     misconceptions: [
 1414:       `using the name ${topic} without explaining its technical meaning`,
 1415:       "giving a generic benefit without applying it to the question",
 1416:       "describing an example that does not demonstrate the concept",
 1417:     ],
 1418:     keywords: [
 1419:       "purpose",
 1420:       "process",
 1421:       "input",
 1422:       "output",
 1423:       "benefit",
 1424:       "limitation",
 1425:     ],
 1426:   };
 1427: }
 1428: 
 1429: function specificDefinition(topic: string): string {
 1430:   return resolveTopicProfile(topic).definition;
 1431: }
 1432: 
 1433: function specificExtendedAnswer(topic: string, contextLabel: string): string {
 1434:   const profile = resolveTopicProfile(topic);
 1435: 
 1436:   const benefitOne = profile.benefits[0];
 1437: 
 1438:   const benefitTwo = profile.benefits[1];
 1439: 
 1440:   const limitationOne = profile.limitations[0];
 1441: 
 1442:   const limitationTwo = profile.limitations[1];
 1443: 
 1444:   return `${topic} could be useful in ${contextLabel} because it can provide ${benefitOne}. It may also support ${benefitTwo}. For example, ${profile.examples[0]}.
 1445: 
 1446: However, ${limitationOne}. In addition, ${limitationTwo}. These limitations mean that the organisation should not adopt the approach without controls, testing and review.
 1447: 
 1448: A suitable alternative or safeguard is to introduce the system gradually, restrict access where appropriate, test it using representative data and maintain a fallback process. Staff or users should also receive guidance so that the technology is used correctly.
 1449: 
 1450: Overall, ${topic} should be used when its benefits clearly match the needs of ${contextLabel} and when the organisation can manage the identified risks. A controlled implementation with monitoring and regular review is more appropriate than unrestricted adoption.`;
 1451: }
 1452: 
 1453: function definitionForTopic(topic: string): string {
 1454:   return specificDefinition(topic);
 1455: }
 1456: 
 1457: 
 1458: type DemoQuestionContent = {
 1459:   question: string;
 1460:   context: string;
 1461:   markPoints: string[];
 1462:   modelAnswer: string;
 1463:   examinerGuidance: string[];
 1464:   misconceptions: string[];
 1465: };
 1466: 
 1467: function createDataRepresentationDemoContent(
 1468:   item: AssessmentBlueprintItem,
 1469:   index: number,
 1470: ): DemoQuestionContent | null {
 1471:   const profile = resolveTopicProfile(item.topicFocus);
 1472:   const variant = index + 1;
 1473: 
 1474:   if (profile.id === "image-representation") {
 1475:     const width = 480 + variant * 160;
 1476:     const height = 320 + variant * 80;
 1477:     const colourDepth = variant % 2 === 0 ? 24 : 8;
 1478:     const pixels = width * height;
 1479:     const bits = pixels * colourDepth;
 1480:     const bytes = bits / 8;
 1481: 
 1482:     if (
 1483:       item.questionType === "calculation" ||
 1484:       item.questionType === "worked-calculation" ||
 1485:       item.questionType === "conversion"
 1486:     ) {
 1487:       return {
 1488:         question: `A bitmap image is ${width} pixels wide and ${height} pixels high. It uses a colour depth of ${colourDepth} bits. Calculate the uncompressed file size in bytes. Show your working.`,
 1489:         context:
 1490:           "Ignore file headers, metadata and compression. Use 8 bits = 1 byte.",
 1491:         markPoints: [
 1492:           `Calculates the number of pixels as ${width} Ã— ${height} = ${pixels}.`,
 1493:           `Multiplies by the colour depth: ${pixels} Ã— ${colourDepth} = ${bits} bits.`,
 1494:           `Divides by 8 to convert bits to bytes.`,
 1495:           `Gives the final answer as ${bytes} bytes.`,
 1496:           "Shows a correct file-size method.",
 1497:           "Uses the correct unit.",
 1498:         ],
 1499:         modelAnswer: `${width} Ã— ${height} = ${pixels} pixels.\n${pixels} Ã— ${colourDepth} = ${bits} bits.\n${bits} Ã· 8 = ${bytes} bytes.\n\nThe uncompressed file size is ${bytes} bytes.`,
 1500:         examinerGuidance: [
 1501:           "Award method marks when the candidate uses width Ã— height Ã— colour depth even if a later arithmetic error occurs.",
 1502:           "Do not award the final conversion mark if a value in bits is labelled as bytes.",
 1503:         ],
 1504:         misconceptions: [
 1505:           "Adding width and height instead of multiplying them to find the number of pixels.",
 1506:           "Forgetting to divide by 8 when converting the file size from bits to bytes.",
 1507:         ],
 1508:       };
 1509:     }
 1510: 
 1511:     if (item.questionType === "multiple-choice") {
 1512:       return {
 1513:         question:
 1514:           "Which statement about bitmap image representation is correct?\n\nA. Resolution is the number of bits used for each pixel.\nB. Colour depth is the total number of pixels in the image.\nC. Increasing resolution usually increases the number of pixels stored.\nD. Increasing colour depth always reduces the file size.",
 1515:         context: "Select one answer.",
 1516:         markPoints: [
 1517:           "Award 1 mark for option C: increasing resolution usually increases the number of pixels stored.",
 1518:         ],
 1519:         modelAnswer: "C",
 1520:         examinerGuidance: [
 1521:           "Award the mark only for option C.",
 1522:           "Do not award a mark for multiple selected options unless C is clearly identified as the final answer.",
 1523:         ],
 1524:         misconceptions: [
 1525:           "Confusing resolution with colour depth.",
 1526:           "Assuming that increasing image quality settings reduces storage requirements.",
 1527:         ],
 1528:       };
 1529:     }
 1530: 
 1531:     if (
 1532:       item.questionType === "extended-response" ||
 1533:       item.questionType === "discuss" ||
 1534:       item.questionType === "evaluate" ||
 1535:       item.questionType === "scenario-application"
 1536:     ) {
 1537:       return {
 1538:         question:
 1539:           "A school is preparing photographs for its website. Evaluate how resolution, colour depth and compression should be chosen to balance image quality, file size and download speed. Reach a justified recommendation.",
 1540:         context:
 1541:           "The website will be viewed on phones and laptops, and some users have slow internet connections.",
 1542:         markPoints: [
 1543:           "Explains that increasing resolution can increase detail but normally increases file size.",
 1544:           "Explains that increasing colour depth can represent more colours but normally increases file size.",
 1545:           "Explains that compression reduces storage and transmission requirements.",
 1546:           "Distinguishes an appropriate use of lossy or lossless compression.",
 1547:           "Applies the discussion to website download speed or bandwidth.",
 1548:           "Recognises that unnecessarily high settings provide limited benefit for the intended display size.",
 1549:           "Uses accurate terminology including pixels, resolution and colour depth.",
 1550:           "Develops a balanced recommendation.",
 1551:           "Links the recommendation to the needs of phone and laptop users.",
 1552:           "Reaches a justified conclusion.",
 1553:           "Considers image quality as well as storage/transmission.",
 1554:           "Maintains a clear line of reasoning.",
 1555:         ],
 1556:         modelAnswer:
 1557:           "The school should use a resolution high enough for the largest display size required, but it should avoid storing far more pixels than the website can use because this increases file size without a visible benefit for most users. A suitable colour depth should preserve realistic colours, but increasing colour depth also increases the number of bits needed for every pixel. For photographic website images, moderate lossy compression is usually appropriate because it can substantially reduce file size and download time while keeping visible quality acceptable. This is particularly important for users on slower connections. The school should therefore resize photographs to their intended display dimensions, use sufficient colour depth for the content and apply controlled compression after checking the visual result.",
 1558:         examinerGuidance: [
 1559:           "Highest-level responses must connect technical choices directly to both image quality and transmission/storage constraints.",
 1560:           "A response that simply states that higher resolution and colour depth are 'better' without considering file size cannot access the highest level.",
 1561:         ],
 1562:         misconceptions: [
 1563:           "Treating resolution and colour depth as the same property.",
 1564:           "Assuming that lossy compression can always reconstruct every discarded detail.",
 1565:         ],
 1566:       };
 1567:     }
 1568: 
 1569:     if (item.questionType === "compare") {
 1570:       return {
 1571:         question:
 1572:           "Compare resolution and colour depth as properties of a bitmap image.",
 1573:         context:
 1574:           "Your answer should explain what each property controls and how changing it can affect file size.",
 1575:         markPoints: [
 1576:           "States that resolution relates to the number of pixels in the image.",
 1577:           "States that colour depth is the number of bits used to represent each pixel's colour.",
 1578:           "Explains that increasing resolution normally increases file size.",
 1579:           "Explains that increasing colour depth normally increases file size.",
 1580:           "Explains that resolution mainly affects spatial detail.",
 1581:           "Explains that colour depth affects the range of colours that can be represented.",
 1582:         ],
 1583:         modelAnswer:
 1584:           "Resolution describes the number of pixels used to represent the image, so a higher resolution can preserve more spatial detail. Colour depth describes the number of bits used for each pixel, so a greater colour depth allows more possible colours. Increasing either property normally increases the uncompressed file size, but they affect different aspects of image quality.",
 1585:         examinerGuidance: [
 1586:           "Award comparison credit only when the response distinguishes what resolution and colour depth represent.",
 1587:           "Do not credit a statement that treats colour depth as the number of pixels.",
 1588:         ],
 1589:         misconceptions: [
 1590:           "Saying that colour depth controls the width and height of an image.",
 1591:           "Saying that resolution is the number of colours available.",
 1592:         ],
 1593:       };
 1594:     }
 1595: 
 1596:     return {
 1597:       question:
 1598:         item.marks === 1
 1599:           ? "State what is meant by the resolution of a bitmap image."
 1600:           : "Explain how resolution and colour depth affect a bitmap image and its uncompressed file size.",
 1601:       context:
 1602:         item.marks === 1
 1603:           ? "Use precise Computer Science terminology."
 1604:           : "Refer to pixels and bits in your answer.",
 1605:       markPoints: [
 1606:         "States that image resolution is the number of pixels used to represent the image.",
 1607:         "States that colour depth is the number of bits used to represent each pixel's colour.",
 1608:         "Explains that increasing resolution normally increases the number of stored pixels.",
 1609:         "Explains that increasing colour depth increases the bits stored for each pixel.",
 1610:         "Links either change to an increase in uncompressed file size.",
 1611:         "Uses accurate terminology.",
 1612:       ],
 1613:       modelAnswer:
 1614:         item.marks === 1
 1615:           ? "Resolution is the number of pixels used to represent an image, commonly described by its width and height in pixels."
 1616:           : "Resolution determines how many pixels are used to represent the image, while colour depth determines how many bits are used for each pixel's colour. Increasing the resolution normally stores more pixels, and increasing the colour depth stores more bits per pixel. Therefore, either change normally increases the uncompressed file size.",
 1617:       examinerGuidance: [
 1618:         "Credit width Ã— height as a valid way to describe image resolution.",
 1619:         "Do not treat resolution and colour depth as interchangeable terms.",
 1620:       ],
 1621:       misconceptions: [
 1622:         "Confusing the number of pixels with the number of bits per pixel.",
 1623:         "Claiming that higher image settings always reduce file size.",
 1624:       ],
 1625:     };
 1626:   }
 1627: 
 1628:   if (profile.id === "sound-representation") {
 1629:     const sampleRate = variant % 2 === 0 ? 22050 : 44100;
 1630:     const bitDepth = variant % 2 === 0 ? 16 : 8;
 1631:     const duration = 5 + variant;
 1632:     const bits = sampleRate * bitDepth * duration;
 1633:     const bytes = bits / 8;
 1634: 
 1635:     if (
 1636:       item.questionType === "calculation" ||
 1637:       item.questionType === "worked-calculation"
 1638:     ) {
 1639:       return {
 1640:         question: `An uncompressed mono sound recording uses a sample rate of ${sampleRate} Hz and a bit depth of ${bitDepth} bits. The recording lasts ${duration} seconds. Calculate the file size in bytes. Show your working.`,
 1641:         context: "Use 8 bits = 1 byte. Ignore file headers and compression.",
 1642:         markPoints: [
 1643:           `Uses ${sampleRate} samples per second.`,
 1644:           `Multiplies sample rate Ã— bit depth Ã— duration to obtain ${bits} bits.`,
 1645:           "Divides by 8 to convert bits to bytes.",
 1646:           `Gives the final answer as ${bytes} bytes.`,
 1647:           "Shows a correct file-size method.",
 1648:           "Uses the correct unit.",
 1649:         ],
 1650:         modelAnswer: `${sampleRate} Ã— ${bitDepth} Ã— ${duration} = ${bits} bits.\n${bits} Ã· 8 = ${bytes} bytes.\n\nThe file size is ${bytes} bytes.`,
 1651:         examinerGuidance: [
 1652:           "Award method credit for sample rate Ã— bit depth Ã— duration even if a later arithmetic error occurs.",
 1653:           "Do not award the byte-conversion mark if the candidate leaves the answer in bits.",
 1654:         ],
 1655:         misconceptions: [
 1656:           "Adding sample rate and bit depth instead of multiplying them.",
 1657:           "Forgetting to include the duration of the recording.",
 1658:         ],
 1659:       };
 1660:     }
 1661: 
 1662:     if (item.questionType === "multiple-choice") {
 1663:       return {
 1664:         question:
 1665:           "Which statement about digital sound is correct?\n\nA. Sample rate is the number of bits used for each sample.\nB. Bit depth controls how many samples are taken each second.\nC. A higher sample rate records the waveform more frequently.\nD. Increasing bit depth always decreases file size.",
 1666:         context: "Select one answer.",
 1667:         markPoints: [
 1668:           "Award 1 mark for option C: a higher sample rate records the waveform more frequently.",
 1669:         ],
 1670:         modelAnswer: "C",
 1671:         examinerGuidance: [
 1672:           "Award the mark only for option C.",
 1673:           "Do not award multiple selections unless C is clearly indicated as the final answer.",
 1674:         ],
 1675:         misconceptions: [
 1676:           "Confusing sample rate with bit depth.",
 1677:           "Assuming higher-quality sampling settings reduce file size.",
 1678:         ],
 1679:       };
 1680:     }
 1681: 
 1682:     if (
 1683:       item.questionType === "extended-response" ||
 1684:       item.questionType === "discuss" ||
 1685:       item.questionType === "evaluate" ||
 1686:       item.questionType === "scenario-application"
 1687:     ) {
 1688:       return {
 1689:         question:
 1690:           "A school is recording speech for an online learning platform. Evaluate how sample rate, bit depth and compression should be chosen to balance sound quality, file size and streaming performance. Reach a justified recommendation.",
 1691:         context:
 1692:           "The recordings contain spoken explanations rather than music, and some students have limited bandwidth.",
 1693:         markPoints: [
 1694:           "Explains the effect of sample rate on how frequently the waveform is measured.",
 1695:           "Explains the effect of bit depth on the precision of stored sample values.",
 1696:           "Links higher sample rate to larger file size.",
 1697:           "Links higher bit depth to larger file size.",
 1698:           "Explains how compression can reduce storage or transmission requirements.",
 1699:           "Applies the discussion to spoken audio rather than music.",
 1700:           "Applies the discussion to students with limited bandwidth.",
 1701:           "Uses accurate terminology.",
 1702:           "Balances quality against file size.",
 1703:           "Reaches a justified recommendation.",
 1704:           "Considers an appropriate compression approach.",
 1705:           "Maintains a clear line of reasoning.",
 1706:         ],
 1707:         modelAnswer:
 1708:           "Because the recordings contain speech, the school does not need unnecessarily high sampling settings intended for high-fidelity music. The sample rate should be high enough to represent speech clearly, and the bit depth should provide sufficient amplitude precision without creating excessive file sizes. Increasing either value increases the amount of data stored. Compression should therefore be used to reduce storage and streaming bandwidth, with settings tested to ensure speech remains clear. A moderate sample rate and bit depth with controlled audio compression would provide a sensible balance for students with slower connections.",
 1709:         examinerGuidance: [
 1710:           "Highest-level responses must connect sampling choices to both sound quality and bandwidth/file-size constraints.",
 1711:           "Do not reward claims that sample rate and bit depth are the same property.",
 1712:         ],
 1713:         misconceptions: [
 1714:           "Saying that bit depth controls the number of samples per second.",
 1715:           "Assuming maximum sampling settings are always the most suitable choice.",
 1716:         ],
 1717:       };
 1718:     }
 1719: 
 1720:     return {
 1721:       question:
 1722:         item.marks === 1
 1723:           ? "State what is meant by the sample rate of a digital sound recording."
 1724:           : "Explain how sample rate and bit depth affect the representation and file size of digital sound.",
 1725:       context: "Use accurate Computer Science terminology.",
 1726:       markPoints: [
 1727:         "States that sample rate is the number of samples taken each second.",
 1728:         "States that bit depth is the number of bits used to store each sample.",
 1729:         "Explains that increasing sample rate records the waveform more frequently.",
 1730:         "Explains that increasing bit depth allows more possible sample values.",
 1731:         "Links higher sampling settings to increased file size.",
 1732:         "Uses accurate terminology.",
 1733:       ],
 1734:       modelAnswer:
 1735:         item.marks === 1
 1736:           ? "Sample rate is the number of samples taken from the analogue sound wave each second."
 1737:           : "Sample rate is the number of samples taken each second, while bit depth is the number of bits used to store each sample. A higher sample rate measures the waveform more frequently and a higher bit depth allows more precise amplitude values. Increasing either setting normally increases the uncompressed file size.",
 1738:       examinerGuidance: [
 1739:         "Accept sampling frequency as an alternative term for sample rate.",
 1740:         "Do not credit an answer that reverses sample rate and bit depth.",
 1741:       ],
 1742:       misconceptions: [
 1743:         "Confusing samples per second with bits per sample.",
 1744:         "Claiming that increasing sample rate decreases file size.",
 1745:       ],
 1746:     };
 1747:   }
 1748: 
 1749:   if (profile.id === "character-representation") {
 1750:     if (item.questionType === "compare" || item.marks >= 4) {
 1751:       return {
 1752:         question:
 1753:           "Compare ASCII and Unicode as character sets. Explain why Unicode is needed even though ASCII is still widely recognised.",
 1754:         context:
 1755:           "Your answer should refer to the range of characters that can be represented.",
 1756:         markPoints: [
 1757:           "States that both character sets map characters to numeric codes.",
 1758:           "Explains that ASCII represents a relatively limited character set.",
 1759:           "Explains that Unicode represents a much larger range of characters.",
 1760:           "Links Unicode to multiple languages or writing systems.",
 1761:           "Recognises that character codes are stored in binary.",
 1762:           "Makes a clear comparison.",
 1763:         ],
 1764:         modelAnswer:
 1765:           "ASCII and Unicode both assign numeric codes to characters so that text can be stored and processed by computers. ASCII supports a relatively limited range of characters, which is sufficient for basic English letters, digits and symbols. Unicode provides codes for a far larger range of characters and writing systems, allowing software to represent text from many languages consistently. The numeric codes are ultimately stored using binary.",
 1766:         examinerGuidance: [
 1767:           "Credit answers that accurately distinguish the character ranges even if they do not quote specific code-space sizes.",
 1768:           "Do not award comparison marks for saying only that Unicode is 'newer' or 'better'.",
 1769:         ],
 1770:         misconceptions: [
 1771:           "Assuming ASCII and Unicode can represent exactly the same character range.",
 1772:           "Treating a character's visible symbol as the same thing as its stored numeric code.",
 1773:         ],
 1774:       };
 1775:     }
 1776: 
 1777:     return {
 1778:       question: "Explain how a character set allows text to be represented by a computer.",
 1779:       context: "You may refer to ASCII or Unicode.",
 1780:       markPoints: [
 1781:         "States that each character is assigned a numeric code.",
 1782:         "Explains that the numeric code can be represented in binary.",
 1783:         "Gives ASCII or Unicode as an example of a character set.",
 1784:         "Explains that systems must use an agreed encoding to interpret the stored value.",
 1785:       ],
 1786:       modelAnswer:
 1787:         "A character set assigns a numeric code to each character. That numeric value is stored as binary, allowing the computer to store and process text. ASCII and Unicode are examples of character sets, with Unicode supporting a much larger range of characters.",
 1788:       examinerGuidance: [
 1789:         "Accept code point as an appropriate term for the numeric value associated with a character.",
 1790:         "Do not award full credit for naming ASCII or Unicode without explaining the mapping to numeric codes.",
 1791:       ],
 1792:       misconceptions: [
 1793:         "Saying that characters are stored directly as shapes rather than encoded values.",
 1794:         "Assuming every character set contains the same characters.",
 1795:       ],
 1796:     };
 1797:   }
 1798: 
 1799:   if (profile.id === "compression") {
 1800:     if (
 1801:       item.questionType === "compare" ||
 1802:       item.questionType === "extended-response" ||
 1803:       item.questionType === "discuss" ||
 1804:       item.questionType === "evaluate" ||
 1805:       item.questionType === "scenario-application"
 1806:     ) {
 1807:       return {
 1808:         question:
 1809:           "A school needs to compress two files: a database backup and a set of photographs for its website. Compare lossy and lossless compression and recommend an appropriate method for each file.",
 1810:         context:
 1811:           "The database backup must be restored exactly. The photographs should download quickly while remaining visually acceptable.",
 1812:         markPoints: [
 1813:           "States that lossless compression preserves all original data.",
 1814:           "States that lossy compression permanently removes some data.",
 1815:           "Explains why lossless compression is appropriate for the database backup.",
 1816:           "Explains why lossy compression may be appropriate for website photographs.",
 1817:           "Links compression to reduced file size.",
 1818:           "Links smaller files to storage or transmission benefits.",
 1819:           "Recognises that excessive lossy compression can reduce quality.",
 1820:           "Applies the answer to both file types.",
 1821:           "Uses accurate terminology.",
 1822:           "Reaches a justified recommendation.",
 1823:           "Maintains a direct comparison.",
 1824:           "Explains why one method is not suitable for every file.",
 1825:         ],
 1826:         modelAnswer:
 1827:           "The database backup should use lossless compression because every original value must be reconstructed exactly when the backup is restored. Lossless compression reduces file size without permanently removing information. The website photographs can use controlled lossy compression because some visual detail can be discarded to achieve a greater reduction in file size, helping pages download more quickly. The school should test the compression level so that the photographs remain visually acceptable. Therefore, lossless compression is appropriate for the database backup, while moderate lossy compression is appropriate for the website photographs.",
 1828:         examinerGuidance: [
 1829:           "Highest-level responses must justify different compression methods for the two different requirements.",
 1830:           "Do not award full marks for saying simply that lossy is 'smaller' and lossless is 'better quality' without explaining data preservation.",
 1831:         ],
 1832:         misconceptions: [
 1833:           "Believing lossless compression permanently removes data.",
 1834:           "Assuming lossy compression is appropriate for a backup that must be restored exactly.",
 1835:         ],
 1836:       };
 1837:     }
 1838: 
 1839:     return {
 1840:       question:
 1841:         "Explain the difference between lossy and lossless compression.",
 1842:       context:
 1843:         "Refer to what happens to the original data and give one suitable use.",
 1844:       markPoints: [
 1845:         "States that lossless compression preserves all original data.",
 1846:         "States that lossy compression permanently removes some data.",
 1847:         "Explains that both are used to reduce file size.",
 1848:         "Gives a suitable example or use for either method.",
 1849:       ],
 1850:       modelAnswer:
 1851:         "Lossless compression reduces file size while allowing the original data to be reconstructed exactly. Lossy compression reduces file size by permanently discarding some data, so the original cannot be recovered perfectly. Lossless compression is suitable for data such as documents or backups, while lossy compression can be suitable for media such as photographs or audio when some quality reduction is acceptable.",
 1852:       examinerGuidance: [
 1853:         "Credit equivalent wording that clearly distinguishes whether all original data can be reconstructed.",
 1854:         "Do not accept the claim that lossless compression always produces a smaller file than lossy compression.",
 1855:       ],
 1856:       misconceptions: [
 1857:         "Saying that lossless compression reduces quality.",
 1858:         "Saying that data removed by lossy compression can always be recovered later.",
 1859:       ],
 1860:     };
 1861:   }
 1862: 
 1863:   return null;
 1864: }
